# Prototype

A prototype is **throwaway code that answers a question**. The question decides the shape.

## Pick a branch

Identify which question is being answered, using the user's prompt, the surrounding code, or by asking if the user is around:

- **"Does this logic / state model feel right?"** → [LOGIC.md](LOGIC.md). Build a single shareable HTML file (free-play buttons plus tabbed guided walkthroughs) that pushes the state machine through cases that are hard to reason about on paper, and that a non-developer can drive.
- **"What should this look like?"** → [UI.md](UI.md). Generate several radically different UI variations on a single route, switchable via a URL search param and a floating bottom bar.

The two branches produce very different artifacts, so getting this wrong wastes the whole prototype. If the question is genuinely ambiguous and the user isn't reachable, default to whichever branch better matches the surrounding code (a backend module → logic; a page or component → UI) and state the assumption at the top of the prototype.

## Rules that apply to both

1. **Throwaway from day one, and clearly marked as such.** Locate the prototype code close to where it will actually be used (next to the module or page it's prototyping for) so context is obvious, but name it so a casual reader can see it's a prototype, not production. For throwaway UI routes, obey whatever routing convention the project already uses; don't invent a new top-level structure.
2. **Trivial to run.** A UI prototype starts from one command in the project's task runner: `pnpm <name>`, `python <path>`, `bun <path>`, etc. A logic demo is a single HTML file the user double-clicks. Either way, no thinking required to start it.
3. **No persistence by default.** State lives in memory. Persistence is the thing the prototype is _checking_, not something it should depend on. If the question explicitly involves a database, hit a scratch DB or a local file with a clear "PROTOTYPE, wipe me" name.
4. **Skip the polish.** No tests, no error handling beyond what makes the prototype _runnable_, no abstractions. The point is to learn something fast.
5. **Surface the state.** After every action (logic) or on every variant switch (UI), print or render the full relevant state so the user can see what changed.
6. **Capture it when done.** Fold any validated decision into the real code, then capture the prototype itself as a **primary source**: commit it to a throwaway branch, out of main, and leave a context pointer to that branch on the implementation issue. Capture the answer too (the verdict and the question it settled) in the issue or a commit. The main branch keeps only the validated decision.

Purpose: How much process a change deserves — maps a change's size and risk to the skills it should run.

# Stakes to ceremony — how much process a change deserves

Not every change needs every skill. Size the change first, then run only the
ceremony its band calls for. Names below are directories under `skills/`;
each runtime invokes them its own way (Claude Code also has `/` shortcuts).
Once the band is known, `ask-matt` gives the detailed route through the
Pocock flow.

## Tell the band

Two questions settle it. **Can it be undone cheaply** (a revert, no data or
users touched)? **Do we know what done looks like** (a test or check you
could write now)? Yes to both → tweak or small. No to the second → big or
fuzzy. No to the first → production / irreversible, whatever the size.

## The table

| Band | Looks like | Ceremony |
|---|---|---|
| **Tweak** | Typo, rename, obvious one-liner, doc fix | Do it, run the checks. No skills. |
| **Small, understood** | One feature or bug, one session, a clear test | `grill-me` to pin the plan (`grill-with-docs` when the repo keeps a glossary), then `tdd`. For a bug that resists a first look, `diagnosing-bugs` in place of `tdd`. |
| **Big or fuzzy** | Several sessions, parallel work, or done is unclear | `to-spec` (+ `to-tickets`), then `plans`; `tdd` per node; `code-review` at every join and reconcile. `wayfinder` when the destination itself is in fog. |
| **Production, security, irreversible** | Migrations, deletes, launches, auth, payments, anything users or data feel | The band above, plus `doubt-driven-development`, `security-and-hardening` and `code-review` on the final diff. As the change calls for it: `blast-radius` (what else breaks), `deprecation-and-migration`, `shipping-and-launch`. |

In doubt, go one band up. Extra ceremony costs an hour; too little costs a
bad deploy.

## Floors at every band

- Run `scripts/run-checks.sh` before calling anything done.
- A choice with a rejected alternative gets a decision note (`decision-log`),
  even for a tweak.
- When a skill repeatedly saves or wastes a session, write it in the work
  item's ledger (`handoff.md`) so the skill gets fixed or pruned; `retro` is
  the tool for turning that note into a change.

## Adopting the skills

For downloaders of this template: do not turn everything on at once.

- **Roll out gradually.** Add a band's skills the first time that band comes
  up, not before.
- **Verification first.** A test suite and `scripts/run-checks.sh` before any
  workflow skill; a workflow skill with no check behind it produces confident
  prose, not working code.
- **Prune what you do not use.** Every model-invoked description is weighed on
  every turn, whether or not it fires. `scripts/check-skill-descriptions.sh`
  bounds the total; stay under it, and make a rarely used skill user-invoked
  rather than carrying its description everywhere.
- **Make a skill earn its place.** Models now do much of what step-by-step
  skills spell out, so measure before keeping one. Pick 3 tasks the skill
  claims to help with and run each three ways, in fresh headless sessions:
  no skill, a dummy file of the same length, and the skill. Keep the skill
  only if it beats both; if it ties the dummy, its length did the work, not
  its content. Decide one skill at a time, never wholesale.

---
name: graphify
description: "Use for any question about a codebase, its architecture, file relationships, or project content — especially when graphify-out/ exists, where the question should be treated as a graphify query first. Turns any input (code, docs, papers, images, videos) into a persistent knowledge graph with god nodes, community detection, and query/path/explain tools."

# /graphify

Turn any folder of files into a navigable knowledge graph with community detection, an honest audit trail, and three outputs: interactive HTML, GraphRAG-ready JSON, and a plain-language GRAPH_REPORT.md.

## Usage

```
/graphify                                             # full pipeline on current directory → Obsidian vault
/graphify <path>                                      # full pipeline on specific path
/graphify https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>           # clone repo then run full pipeline on it
/graphify https://github.com/<owner>/<repo> --branch <branch>  # clone a specific branch
/graphify <url1> <url2> ...                           # clone multiple repos, build each, merge into one cross-repo graph
/graphify <path> --mode deep                          # thorough extraction, richer INFERRED edges
/graphify <path> --update                             # incremental - re-extract only new/changed files
/graphify <path> --directed                            # build directed graph (preserves edge direction: source→target)
/graphify <path> --whisper-model medium                # use a larger Whisper model for better transcription accuracy
/graphify <path> --cluster-only                       # rerun clustering on existing graph
/graphify <path> --no-viz                             # skip visualization, just report + JSON
/graphify <path> --html                               # (HTML is generated by default - this flag is a no-op)
/graphify <path> --svg                                # also export graph.svg (embeds in Notion, GitHub)
/graphify <path> --graphml                            # export graph.graphml (Gephi, yEd)
/graphify <path> --neo4j                              # generate graphify-out/cypher.txt for Neo4j
/graphify <path> --neo4j-push bolt://localhost:7687   # push directly to Neo4j
/graphify <path> --mcp                                # start MCP stdio server for agent access
/graphify <path> --watch                              # watch folder, auto-rebuild on code changes (no LLM needed)
/graphify <path> --wiki                               # build agent-crawlable wiki (index.md + one article per community)
/graphify <path> --obsidian --obsidian-dir ~/vaults/my-project  # write vault to custom path (e.g. existing vault)
/graphify add <url>                                   # fetch URL, save to ./raw, update graph
/graphify add <url> --author "Name"                   # tag who wrote it
/graphify add <url> --contributor "Name"              # tag who added it to the corpus
/graphify query "<question>"                          # BFS traversal - broad context
/graphify query "<question>" --dfs                    # DFS - trace a specific path
/graphify query "<question>" --budget 1500            # cap answer at N tokens
/graphify path "AuthModule" "Database"                # shortest path between two concepts
/graphify explain "SwinTransformer"                   # plain-language explanation of a node
```

## What graphify is for

Drop any folder of code, docs, papers, images, or video into graphify and get a queryable knowledge graph. Persistent across sessions, honest audit trail (EXTRACTED/INFERRED/AMBIGUOUS), community detection surfaces cross-document connections you wouldn't think to ask about.

## What You Must Do When Invoked

If the user invoked `/graphify --help` or `/graphify -h` (with no other arguments), print the contents of the `## Usage` section above verbatim and stop. Do not run any commands, do not detect files, do not default the path to `.`. Just print the Usage block and return.

**Fast path — existing graph:** Before doing anything else, check whether `graphify-out/graph.json` exists. The expected location is `graphify-out/graph.json` relative to the **current working directory** (i.e. the project root where you are running commands). If it exists AND the user's request is a natural-language question about the codebase (e.g. "How does X work?", "What calls Y?", "Trace the data flow through Z") and NOT an explicit rebuild command (`--update`, `--cluster-only`, or a bare path/URL that implies fresh extraction): **skip Steps 1–5 entirely and jump straight to `## For /graphify query`.** Run `graphify query "<question>"` immediately. Do not run detect. Do not check corpus size. Do not ask the user to narrow. The graph is already built — use it.

If no path was given, use `.` (current directory). Do not ask the user for a path.

If the path argument starts with `https://github.com/` or `http://github.com/`, treat it as a GitHub URL - run Step 0 before anything else, then continue with the resolved local path.

Follow these steps in order. Do not skip steps.

### Step 0 - GitHub repos and multi-path merge (only if a URL or several paths)

Only when the path is one or more `https://github.com/...` URLs, or several local subfolders to merge. See `references/github-and-merge.md` for the clone, cross-repo merge, and monorepo flow, then continue with the resolved local path. A plain local path skips this step.

### Step 1 - Ensure graphify is installed

```bash
# Detect the correct Python interpreter (handles uv tool, pipx, venv, system installs)
PYTHON=""
GRAPHIFY_BIN=$(which graphify 2>/dev/null)
# 1. uv tool installs — most reliable on modern Mac/Linux
if [ -z "$PYTHON" ] && command -v uv >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    _UV_PY=$(uv tool run graphifyy python -c "import sys; print(sys.executable)" 2>/dev/null)
    if [ -n "$_UV_PY" ]; then PYTHON="$_UV_PY"; fi
fi
# 2. Read shebang from graphify binary (pipx and direct pip installs)
if [ -z "$PYTHON" ] && [ -n "$GRAPHIFY_BIN" ]; then
    _SHEBANG=$(head -1 "$GRAPHIFY_BIN" | tr -d '#!')
    case "$_SHEBANG" in
        *[!a-zA-Z0-9/_.-]*) ;;
        *) "$_SHEBANG" -c "import graphify" 2>/dev/null && PYTHON="$_SHEBANG" ;;
    esac
fi
# 3. Fall back to python3
if [ -z "$PYTHON" ]; then PYTHON="python3"; fi
if ! "$PYTHON" -c "import graphify" 2>/dev/null; then
    if command -v uv >/dev/null 2>&1; then
        uv tool install --upgrade graphifyy -q 2>&1 | tail -3
        _UV_PY=$(uv tool run graphifyy python -c "import sys; print(sys.executable)" 2>/dev/null)
        if [ -n "$_UV_PY" ]; then PYTHON="$_UV_PY"; fi
    else
        "$PYTHON" -m pip install graphifyy -q 2>/dev/null \
          || "$PYTHON" -m pip install graphifyy -q --break-system-packages 2>&1 | tail -3
    fi
fi
# Write interpreter path for all subsequent steps (persists across invocations)
mkdir -p graphify-out
"$PYTHON" -c "import sys; open('graphify-out/.graphify_python', 'w', encoding='utf-8').write(sys.executable)"
# Save scan root so `graphify update` (no args) knows where to look next time
echo "$(cd INPUT_PATH && pwd)" > graphify-out/.graphify_root
```

If the import succeeds, print nothing and move straight to Step 2.

**In every subsequent bash block, replace `python3` with `$(cat graphify-out/.graphify_python)` to use the correct interpreter.**

### Step 2 - Detect files

```bash
$(cat graphify-out/.graphify_python) -c "
import json
from graphify.detect import detect
from pathlib import Path
result = detect(Path('INPUT_PATH'))
print(json.dumps(result, ensure_ascii=False))
" > graphify-out/.graphify_detect.json
```

Replace INPUT_PATH with the actual path the user provided. Do NOT cat or print the JSON - read it silently and present a clean summary instead:

```
Corpus: X files · ~Y words
  code:     N files (.py .ts .go ...)
  docs:     N files (.md .txt ...)
  papers:   N files (.pdf ...)
  images:   N files
  video:    N files (.mp4 .mp3 ...)
```

Omit any category with 0 files from the summary.

Then act on it:
- If `total_files` is 0: stop with "No supported files found in [path]."
- If `skipped_sensitive` is non-empty: mention file count skipped, not the file names.
- If `total_words` > 2,000,000 OR `total_files` > 500: show the warning. Then compute the top 5 first-level subdirectories by file count:
  - Read `scan_root` from the detect JSON (always an absolute path to the resolved INPUT_PATH).
  - Concatenate all file lists across all types (`code`, `document`, `paper`, `image`, `video`).
  - Filter out any path that starts with `scan_root + "/graphify-out/"` to exclude converted sidecars.
  - For each file, strip the `scan_root` prefix and take the first path component. Files directly in `scan_root` with no subdirectory count as `(root)`.
  - If all files are in `(root)` with no subdirectories, do not ask to narrow — no subfolders exist. Instead suggest `--no-cluster` to skip the expensive clustering step and proceed.
  - Otherwise rank by count, show the top 5 with file counts, then ask which subfolder to run on. Wait for the user's answer before proceeding.
- Otherwise: proceed directly to Step 2.5 if video files were detected, or Step 3 if not.

### Step 2.5 - Video and audio (only if video files detected)

Skip this step entirely if `detect` returned zero `video` files. When the corpus has video or audio, see `references/transcribe.md` to transcribe them to text first, then treat the transcripts as doc files in Step 3.

### Step 3 - Extract entities and relationships

**Before starting:** note whether `--mode deep` was given. You must pass `DEEP_MODE=true` to every subagent in Step B2 if it was. Track this from the original invocation - do not lose it.

This step has two parts: **structural extraction** (deterministic, free) and **semantic extraction** (LLM, costs tokens).

**Before dispatching subagents:** check whether `GEMINI_API_KEY` or `GOOGLE_API_KEY` is set. If neither is set, print this one-liner to the user:
> Tip: set `GEMINI_API_KEY` or `GOOGLE_API_KEY` to use Gemini for semantic extraction (`pip install 'graphifyy[gemini]'`).

Print it once, then continue. If `GEMINI_API_KEY` or `GOOGLE_API_KEY` IS set, use `graphify.llm.extract_corpus_parallel(files, backend="gemini")` for semantic extraction instead of dispatching Claude subagents. The default Gemini model is `gemini-3-flash-preview`; set `GRAPHIFY_GEMINI_MODEL` or pass `--model` in headless CLI flows to override it.

> **No other API keys are read.** If `GEMINI_API_KEY`/`GOOGLE_API_KEY` are unset, fall straight through to Claude Code subagent dispatch (Part B below) — the host session itself is the LLM. graphify does **not** read `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, or any other provider key from the environment. If a host agent prompts the user for `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` to run extraction, that prompt is a misread of this skill — ignore it and dispatch subagents as written.

**Run Part A (AST) and Part B (semantic) in parallel. Dispatch all semantic subagents AND start AST extraction in the same message. Both can run simultaneously since they operate on different file types. Merge results in Part C as before.**

Note: Parallelizing AST + semantic saves 5-15s on large corpora. AST is deterministic and fast; start it while subagents are processing docs/papers.

#### Part A - Structural extraction for code files

For any code files detected, run AST extraction in parallel with Part B subagents:

```bash
$(cat graphify-out/.graphify_python) -c "
import sys, json
from graphify.extract import collect_files, extract
from pathlib import Path
import json

code_files = []
detect = json.loads(Path('graphify-out/.graphify_detect.json').read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))
for f in detect.get('files', {}).get('code', []):
    code_files.extend(collect_files(Path(f)) if Path(f).is_dir() else [Path(f)])

if code_files:
    result = extract(code_files, cache_root=Path('.'))
    Path('graphify-out/.graphify_ast.json').write_text(json.dumps(result, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False), encoding=\"utf-8\")
    print(f'AST: {len(result[\"nodes\"])} nodes, {len(result[\"edges\"])} edges')
else:
    Path('graphify-out/.graphify_ast.json').write_text(json.dumps({'nodes':[],'edges':[],'input_tokens':0,'output_tokens':0}, ensure_ascii=False), encoding=\"utf-8\")
    print('No code files - skipping AST extraction')
"
```

#### Part B - Semantic extraction (parallel subagents)

**Fast path:** If detection found zero docs, papers, and images (code-only corpus), skip Part B entirely and go straight to Part C. AST handles code - there is nothing for semantic subagents to do.

**MANDATORY: You MUST use the Agent tool here. Reading files yourself one-by-one is forbidden - it is 5-10x slower. If you do not use the Agent tool you are doing this wrong.**

Before dispatching subagents, print a timing estimate:
- Load `total_words` and file counts from `graphify-out/.graphify_detect.json`
- Estimate agents needed: `ceil(uncached_non_code_files / 22)` (chunk size is 20-25)
- Estimate time: ~45s per agent batch (they run in parallel, so total ≈ 45s × ceil(agents/parallel_limit))
- Print: "Semantic extraction: ~N files → X agents, estimated ~Ys"

**Step B0 - Check extraction cache first**

Before dispatching any subagents, check which files already have cached extraction results:

```bash
$(cat graphify-out/.graphify_python) -c "
import json
from graphify.cache import check_semantic_cache
from pathlib import Path

detect = json.loads(Path('graphify-out/.graphify_detect.json').read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))
all_files = [f for files in detect['files'].values() for f in files]

cached_nodes, cached_edges, cached_hyperedges, uncached = check_semantic_cache(all_files)

if cached_nodes or cached_edges or cached_hyperedges:
    Path('graphify-out/.graphify_cached.json').write_text(json.dumps({'nodes': cached_nodes, 'edges': cached_edges, 'hyperedges': cached_hyperedges}, ensure_ascii=False), encoding=\"utf-8\")
Path('graphify-out/.graphify_uncached.txt').write_text('\n'.join(uncached), encoding=\"utf-8\")
print(f'Cache: {len(all_files)-len(uncached)} files hit, {len(uncached)} files need extraction')
"
```

Only dispatch subagents for files listed in `graphify-out/.graphify_uncached.txt`. If all files are cached, skip to Part C directly.

**Step B1 - Split into chunks**

Load files from `graphify-out/.graphify_uncached.txt`. Split into chunks of 20-25 files each. Each image gets its own chunk (vision needs separate context). When splitting, group files from the same directory together so related artifacts land in the same chunk and cross-file relationships are more likely to be extracted.

**Step B2 - Dispatch ALL subagents in a single message (Codex)**

> **Codex platform:** Uses `spawn_agent` + `wait_agent` + `close_agent` instead of the Agent tool.
> Requires `multi_agent = true` under `[features]` in `~/.codex/config.toml`.
> If `spawn_agent` is unavailable, tell the user to add that config and restart Codex.

Call `spawn_agent` once per chunk — ALL in the same response so they run in parallel. Build the message by wrapping the extraction prompt in task-delegation framing:

```
spawn_agent(agent_type="worker", message="Your task is to perform the following. Follow the instructions below exactly.\n\n<agent-instructions>\n[extraction prompt, with FILE_LIST, CHUNK_NUM, TOTAL_CHUNKS, DEEP_MODE substituted]\n</agent-instructions>\n\nExecute this now. Output ONLY the structured JSON response.")
```

/bin/zsh -lc "sed -n '1,300p' work/html-kit/prototype/AUTHORING.md && sed -n '1,260p' work/html-kit/prototype/DIAGRAMS.md && sed -n '1,420p' docs/setup-guide.html" in /Users/kashif/Developer/experiments/ai-workspace-template

# Writing a page on the HTML kit

You write content. The kit (`kit/kit.css`, `kit/kit.js`) does all design and
behaviour. You never need to open the kit files.

## Hard rules

1. No `<style>` element and no `style="…"` attribute.
2. No `<script>` other than the kit's one line below.
3. Diagrams are Mermaid (`<pre class="mermaid">`) or a hand-drawn
   `<svg class="diagram">`. In the SVG, no colour or font attributes
   (`fill=`, `stroke=`, `font-*=`) and no `<defs>`: the kit colours it.
4. Use only the elements and classes listed here. If the content needs
   something not listed, use plain semantic HTML (`<p>`, `<ul>`, `<table>`, …)
   and say so when you hand the page back.

## Skeleton

```html
<!doctype html>
<html lang="en">
<head>
<meta charset="utf-8">
<meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1">
<title>Page title</title>
<link rel="stylesheet" href="kit/kit.css">
<script src="kit/kit.js" defer></script>
</head>
<body class="doc">   <!-- or class="deck" -->
<main>
  …content…
</main>
<footer><p>Owner · last updated · source links</p></footer>   <!-- optional, doc only -->
</body>
</html>
```

Adjust the `kit/` path so it points at the kit folder from where the page lives.

**Doc** (`body.doc`): a scrolling page. Inside `<main>`: an `<h1>`, an optional
`.lede`, then one `<section>` per topic, each starting with an `<h2>`.
Use `<h3>` for sub-topics inside a section.

**Deck** (`body.deck`): slides. Inside `<main>`: one `<section class="slide">`
per slide. Appendix slides get `class="slide backup"`; they come last and sit
outside the slide count. The kit adds the progress bar, footer, and keyboard
navigation; don't write them. Use `<h1>` on the title slide, `<h2>` elsewhere;
a subtitle under the title is a `.lede`. Everything on this sheet works on
slides too (eyebrow, tags, tables, figures, cards). Keep one idea per slide:
if it doesn't fit 1280×800, split it.

## Text roles

| Write | For |
|---|---|
| `<p class="eyebrow">` | small label above a heading |
| `<p class="lede">` | the opening paragraph; larger text |
| `<p class="note">` | small grey aside, sources, caveats |
| `<p class="callout">` | one key sentence, ruled on the left; open with `<b>` |
| `<div class="callout">` | a ruled box with an `<h3>` then `<p>`s or a `<ul>` (key steps, warnings) |
| `<div class="statement"><p>…</p><p>…</p></div>` | a few very large lines (closing slide); every second line is coloured |
| `<p class="meta">` | small monospace line, e.g. a source under a card |
| `<b>` | a bold lead-in at the start of a point |
| `<em>`, `<strong>` | emphasis in running text |
| `<code>` | file names, commands, identifiers |

## Components

**Tag** — short inline label: `<span class="tag ok">[measured]</span>`.
Tones: `ok` (green), `info` (blue), `warn` (amber), `bad` (red); no tone = grey.
Pick tones by meaning and keep the mapping the same across the page.

**Numbered points** — `<ol class="points"><li><b>Lead.</b> Rest of the point.</li>…</ol>`.

**Big numbers** —
```html
<div class="stats">
  <div class="stat"><b>−1.5%</b><p>what the number measures</p></div>
</div>
```

**Cards in a grid** — the grid picks the column count from the width.
```html
<div class="grid">
  <div class="card ok">
    <h3>Title <small>optional subtitle</small></h3>
    <p>Body text.</p>          <!-- or a <ul> of short items -->
    <p class="meta">Source · figure</p>
  </div>
</div>
```
Card accents (coloured top edge): `accent`, `ink`, `ok`, `info`, `warn`, `bad`,
or none. Use `<div class="grid small">` for four or more short cards
(a heading and one sentence each); three or fewer cards, or cards with
several sentences, use the plain `grid`.
To caption a grid (cards that are a figure, e.g. two layers side by side),
wrap it like a table: `<figure><div class="grid">…</div><figcaption>…</figcaption></figure>`.

**Rows** — a coloured ladder (tiers, levels, verdicts). Each row has exactly
three parts: a label, a description, and a `<b>` verdict coloured by the row's tone.
```html
<ul class="rows">
  <li class="ok"><span class="tag ok">[measured]</span> <span>What it means</span> <b>What to do</b></li>
  <li class="bad"><span class="tag bad">[hype]</span> <span>What it means</span> <b>What to do</b></li>
</ul>
```
Tones as for tags, plus `accent`.

**Table** — a plain `<table>` with `<thead>`/`<tbody>`. The first column is
bold. The kit adds the scroll box; don't wrap it.

**Caption on a table or code block** — wrap it in a `<figure>`, same as a diagram:
`<figure><table>…</table><figcaption>What it shows.</figcaption></figure>`.
Don't fake a caption with `.note`.

**Code block** — commands, file contents, output. Keep line breaks as written;
escape `<`, `>` and `&`. Never `<p>` + `<br>` for code.
```html
<pre><code>git clone https://example.com/repo.git
cd repo &amp;&amp; ./setup.sh</code></pre>
```

**Steps** — numbered steps where a step holds a code block (plain `<ol>` for
one-line steps). Each `<li>` holds an `<h3>`, then any `<p>` and code blocks.
```html
<ol class="steps">
  <li><h3>Install the tools</h3>
    <pre><code>brew install jq gh</code></pre></li>
  <li><h3>Log in</h3><p>Pick HTTPS when asked.</p>
    <pre><code>gh auth login</code></pre></li>
</ol>
```

**Contents** — a doc with five or more sections gets a list of links after the
lede; give each `<section>` an `id`.
```html
<nav class="toc"><ol><li><a href="#install">Install</a></li>…</ol></nav>
…
<section id="install"><h2>Install</h2>…</section>
```

**Diagram** — a hand-drawn `<svg class="diagram">` or a `<pre class="mermaid">`,
inside a `<figure>` with a `<figcaption>`. Page has a diagram: read
`DIAGRAMS.md` first (choosing, drawing, sizing, the render check).

**Links** — plain `<a href>`. Quotes and dashes as characters or entities.
A list of links (further reading, related pages) is a plain `<ul>`, one link per
`<li>` with a few words on what it covers; not a `.note` with `·` between links.
Relative links resolve from where the page *now* lives: if you moved or
rebuilt a page in another folder, re-point every relative `href` and the
`kit/` path (e.g. `../README.md` from `docs/`); `check-links.sh` confirms.

## Check before handing back

- `grep -cE '<style|style=|fill=|stroke=|font-[a-z]+=|<defs' page.html` prints `0`.
- `grep '<svg' page.html | grep -vc 'class="diagram"'` prints `0`.
- The only `<script>` is the kit line in `<head>`.
- Every class you used appears on this sheet or in `DIAGRAMS.md`.
- `check-links.sh page.html` (next to `kit/`) passes: the kit path and every
  relative `href`/`src` resolve.
- Page has any figure: run `render-check.sh page.html` and open every shot
  (`DIAGRAMS.md` → "Render check"). Passing the greps says nothing about how a
  figure looks.
# Diagrams on the HTML kit

Read this only when a page has a diagram. `AUTHORING.md` holds everything
else; its hard rules still apply (no colour or font attributes in an SVG,
no `<defs>`).

## Choosing

Default to hand-drawn SVG. Mermaid only for a short chain or sequence (about
five boxes or fewer, few branches) that the render check shows readable at both
widths; Mermaid lays itself out and often renders tiny on a wide doc page. Draw
SVG whenever the layout carries the meaning (a ring, a centre, a timeline,
feedback arcs), and switch to SVG the moment a Mermaid diagram renders small or
tangled. Good diagrams beat small files.

## Hand-drawn diagram

You place the shapes; the kit colours them, in light and dark.
```html
<figure>
  <svg class="diagram" viewBox="0 0 520 120" role="img" aria-label="Full description of the diagram">
    <rect x="20"  y="40" width="120" height="40" rx="5"/><text x="80"  y="60">Plan</text>
    <rect x="200" y="40" width="120" height="40" rx="5"/><text x="260" y="60">Build</text>
    <rect class="solid" x="380" y="40" width="120" height="40" rx="20"/><text class="inverse" x="440" y="60">Ship</text>
    <line class="arrow" x1="140" y1="60" x2="199" y2="60"/>
    <line class="arrow" x1="320" y1="60" x2="379" y2="60"/>
    <path class="arrow dash accent" d="M 260 80 C 260 115, 80 115, 80 81"/>
    <text class="small accent" x="170" y="93">rework</text>
  </svg>
  <figcaption>What the diagram shows.</figcaption>
</figure>
```
- Shapes (`rect`, `circle`, `ellipse`, `polygon`) are outlined boxes; lines
  (`line`, `path`, `polyline`) are plain strokes. `<text>` is centred on its
  `x`,`y`, both across and up-down, so put it at the middle of its box.
- Classes: `arrow` (arrowhead at the line's end; it takes the line's colour),
  `dash`, a tone (`accent`, `ok`, `info`, `warn`, `bad`) to colour a line, shape
  or text, `solid` (shape filled with its tone; accent if none), and on text,
  `small` (monospace edge label) and `inverse` (text on a `solid` shape).
- On a doc page the kit sizes the diagram from its `viewBox` width: on a wide
  screen text lands at body size, on a phone the diagram fills the column
  (about 340px), so text shrinks by 340 ÷ viewBox width. Keep the `viewBox`
  about 520–600 wide (stack or wrap the layout to fit) and crop it to the
  drawing, with no empty margins. Default text is 16 units, `small` is 14;
  size boxes for that. Leave about 10 units of clearance between labels and lines.
  A layout that can't fit 600 wide keeps its labels at 12px on a phone and
  scrolls sideways inside its figure; say so in the caption, or split it into
  smaller diagrams.
- Always give `role="img"` and an `aria-label` that says what the picture says.

## Mermaid

```html
<figure>
  <pre class="mermaid">
flowchart LR
  A[Plan] --> B[Build] --> C[Verify]
  C -. failures .-> B
  </pre>
  <figcaption>What the diagram shows, in one or two sentences.</figcaption>
</figure>
```
Keep labels short. Don't set colours or styles in the Mermaid source; the kit
themes it for light and dark. Escape `<`, `>` and `&` inside diagram text.

## Render check

Run `render-check.sh page.html` (it sits next to the `kit/` folder; four shots
per figure: 1280 and 390 wide, light and dark) and open every shot. Each must
show labels readable at real size, nothing clipped or overlapping, and the
figure sized in proportion to the page. Fix and re-run until all pass; passing
the greps says nothing about how a diagram looks.

      <text class="sub" x="605" y="148">single source of truth</text>

      <line x1="210" y1="45"  x2="470" y2="120" stroke="#7c83ff" stroke-width="2" marker-end="url(#arr)"/>
      <line x1="210" y1="130" x2="470" y2="130" stroke="#7c83ff" stroke-width="2" marker-end="url(#arr)"/>
      <line x1="210" y1="215" x2="470" y2="140" stroke="#7c83ff" stroke-width="2" marker-end="url(#arr)"/>
      <text class="sub" x="340" y="70">symlink</text>
    </svg>
    <div class="figcap">Figure 2 — Claude Code, Codex (AGENTS.md), and Gemini all resolve to one file</div>
  </div>

  <!-- ============================================= -->
  <h2 id="quickstart">03 · Quick start</h2>
  <p>Five steps from clone to a working workspace. Steps 1–3 are mechanical; step 4 is where you make it
  <em>your</em> project.</p>

  <div class="fig">
    <svg viewBox="0 0 920 130" role="img" aria-label="Setup flow: clone, bootstrap, configure, fill in, build">
      <style>
        .fb{fill:#1d2138;stroke:#2b3052}
        .ft{font:13px var(--sans,sans-serif);fill:#e7e9f3;text-anchor:middle;font-weight:600}
        .fn{font:11px var(--mono,monospace);fill:#22d3ee;text-anchor:middle}
      </style>
      <defs>
        <marker id="fa" markerWidth="10" markerHeight="10" refX="7" refY="3" orient="auto">
          <path d="M0,0 L7,3 L0,6 Z" fill="#7c83ff"/>
        </marker>
      </defs>
      <g>
        <rect class="fb" x="10"  y="35" width="150" height="60" rx="12"/>
        <text class="fn" x="85"  y="58">01</text><text class="ft" x="85"  y="78">Clone &amp; re-init</text>
        <rect class="fb" x="200" y="35" width="150" height="60" rx="12"/>
        <text class="fn" x="275" y="58">02</text><text class="ft" x="275" y="78">Bootstrap</text>
        <rect class="fb" x="390" y="35" width="150" height="60" rx="12"/>
        <text class="fn" x="465" y="58">03</text><text class="ft" x="465" y="78">Wire agents</text>
        <rect class="fb" x="580" y="35" width="150" height="60" rx="12"/>
        <text class="fn" x="655" y="58">04</text><text class="ft" x="655" y="78">Fill placeholders</text>
        <rect class="fb" x="770" y="35" width="140" height="60" rx="12" stroke="#34d399"/>
        <text class="fn" x="840" y="58" fill="#34d399">05</text><text class="ft" x="840" y="78">Build</text>
      </g>
      <line x1="160" y1="65" x2="200" y2="65" stroke="#7c83ff" stroke-width="2" marker-end="url(#fa)"/>
      <line x1="350" y1="65" x2="390" y2="65" stroke="#7c83ff" stroke-width="2" marker-end="url(#fa)"/>
      <line x1="540" y1="65" x2="580" y2="65" stroke="#7c83ff" stroke-width="2" marker-end="url(#fa)"/>
      <line x1="730" y1="65" x2="770" y2="65" stroke="#7c83ff" stroke-width="2" marker-end="url(#fa)"/>
    </svg>
    <div class="figcap">Figure 3 — The setup pipeline</div>
  </div>

  <div class="step"><div class="num">1</div><div class="body">
    <h3>Create your workspace from the template</h3>
    <p>On GitHub, click <strong>“Use this template”</strong>, or clone and reset git history:</p>
    <pre><code>git clone https://github.com/&lt;you&gt;/ai-workspace-template.git &lt;project&gt;-workspace
cd &lt;project&gt;-workspace
rm -rf .git &amp;&amp; git init</code></pre>
  </div></div>

  <div class="step"><div class="num">2</div><div class="body">
    <h3>Bootstrap the local files</h3>
    <p>Run the setup script — it runs a dependency preflight, then creates <code>.env</code>, the agent symlinks,
    <code>repos/README.md</code>, and the per-user config copies (idempotent). Add <code>--clone-repos</code> for
    a multi-repo workspace. Missing tools? Follow <a href="runbooks/dependencies.md">runbooks/dependencies.md</a>.</p>
    <pre><code>./scripts/setup.sh                 # creates .env, symlinks, repos/README.md
./scripts/check-workspace-structure.sh   # verify symlinks resolve, dirs present</code></pre>
  </div></div>

  <div class="step"><div class="num">3</div><div class="body">
    <h3>Wire up agents &amp; MCP</h3>
    <p>Copy the per-user config templates (all gitignored), then authenticate. Run the check, then follow
    <a href="runbooks/authentication.md">runbooks/authentication.md</a> for anything missing; per-runtime MCP
    detail is in <code>docs/mcp-setup.md</code>. The YouTube transcript MCP server is workspace-local and
    credential-free; install <code>yt-dlp</code> if you use it.</p>
    <pre><code>cp -n .env.example .env
cp -n .mcp.json.example .mcp.json
cp -n .claude/settings.json.example .claude/settings.local.json
./scripts/check-service-access.sh                  # reports gaps + fix commands
gh auth login                                      # if not already authenticated</code></pre>
  </div></div>

  <div class="step"><div class="num">4</div><div class="body">
    <h3>Make it your project</h3>
    <p>Replace every <code>&lt;…&gt;</code> placeholder and resolve the <code>TODO</code> / “Fill in” comments —
    start with <code>CONTEXT.md</code> (it loads into every agent session). Find what's left:</p>
    <pre><code>grep -rIn --exclude-dir=.git -e '&lt;[a-z-]\+&gt;' -e 'TODO' -e 'Fill in:' .</code></pre>
    <ul class="tight">
      <li><strong>CONTEXT.md</strong> — name, purpose, single- vs multi-repo model</li>
      <li><strong>docs/repos-registry.md</strong> — one entry per product repo</li>
      <li><strong>docs/service-access.md</strong> — your GitHub username; add a section per service</li>
      <li><strong>SPEC.md</strong> — product intent (or delete for a pure coordination layer)</li>
      <li><strong>work/</strong> — prune the maintainer's shipped work history
        (<code>git rm -r work/*/</code>); it's a worked example, not machinery — see
        <a href="template-usage.md">template-usage.md</a> §5</li>
    </ul>
  </div></div>

  <div class="step"><div class="num">5</div><div class="body">
    <h3>Start building</h3>
    <p>Decide on the optional bits: <strong>graphify</strong> is the core MCP server (wired by default;
    it also adds <code>.gemini/settings.json</code> / <code>.opencode/plugins/graphify.js</code>).
    <strong>YouTube transcript</strong> MCP is an opt-in fragment under
    <code>mcp-fragments/</code>, loaded per session — see <code>docs/mcp-setup.md</code>; GitHub goes
    through the required <code>gh</code> CLI (no MCP server). Delete what you don't use.
    Add reusable workflows under <code>skills/</code> and list them in <code>CONTEXT.md</code>.
    The template ships four: <code>checkpoint</code> (session wrap-up), <code>onboard-repo</code>
    (bring a repo in), <code>rlm</code> (query huge contexts), and <code>decision-log</code> —
    capture the <em>why</em> behind a decision (<code>/decision</code>) as an ephemeral note in
    <code>work/&lt;proj&gt;/decisions.md</code>, promoted to a committed ADR under <code>docs/adr/</code>
    for lasting-weight calls. See the <strong>Decision Records</strong> section of <code>CONTEXT.md</code>.
    <strong>Checks:</strong> <code>scripts/run-checks.sh</code> runs every workspace check; on GitHub,
    <code>.github/workflows/checks.yml</code> runs it on each push (delete the file to opt out), and
    <code>git config core.hooksPath scripts/git-hooks</code> opts you in to a pre-commit run of the fast set.</p>
  </div></div>

  <p style="margin-top:18px">Deeper reference: <a href="template-usage.md">docs/template-usage.md</a> ·
  <a href="workspace-setup.md">docs/workspace-setup.md</a> ·
  <a href="workspace-structure.md">docs/workspace-structure.md</a></p>

  <!-- ============================================= -->
  <h2 id="global">04 · Global vs. workspace-local setup</h2>
  <p>Setup splits into two layers. Do the <span class="tag g">GLOBAL</span> layer once per machine; the
  <span class="tag l">LOCAL</span> layer once per clone. The local layer depends on the global one (the MCP
  token and the <code>gh</code> login).</p>

  <div class="fig">
    <svg viewBox="0 0 820 360" role="img" aria-label="Global machine config versus workspace-local files">
      <style>
        .hd{font:14px var(--sans,sans-serif);font-weight:700;text-anchor:middle}
        .it{font:12.5px var(--mono,monospace);fill:#cfd4ff}
        .ds{font:11px var(--mono,monospace);fill:#a3a8c3}
      </style>
      <rect x="20"  y="20" width="370" height="320" rx="14" fill="#241d0c" stroke="#5a4a1e"/>
      <text class="hd" x="205" y="48" fill="#fbbf24">GLOBAL — once per machine</text>
      <text class="ds" x="40" y="70">~/ home directory · OS keychain · never in git</text>

      <text class="it" x="40" y="104">agent CLIs on PATH</text>
      <text class="ds" x="40" y="120">claude · codex · opencode · gemini</text>
      <text class="it" x="40" y="148">gh CLI login (required)</text>
      <text class="ds" x="40" y="164">all GitHub operations · gh auth status</text>
      <text class="it" x="40" y="236">~/.codex/config.toml</text>
      <text class="ds" x="40" y="252">Codex MCP registration (optional)</text>
      <text class="it" x="40" y="280">~/.gemini/settings.json</text>
      <text class="ds" x="40" y="296">Gemini MCP registration (optional)</text>
      <text class="it" x="40" y="324">OS keychain</text>

      <rect x="430" y="20" width="370" height="320" rx="14" fill="#0c2025" stroke="#194a52"/>
      <text class="hd" x="615" y="48" fill="#22d3ee">LOCAL — once per clone</text>
      <text class="ds" x="450" y="70">inside the repo · copy the .example files</text>

      <text class="it" x="450" y="104">.env.example → .env</text>
      <text class="ds" x="450" y="120">non-secret identifiers only</text>
      <text class="it" x="450" y="148">.mcp.json.example → .mcp.json</text>
      <text class="ds" x="450" y="164">Claude Code project MCP</text>
      <text class="it" x="450" y="192">.claude/settings.json.example</text>
      <text class="ds" x="450" y="208">→ .claude/settings.local.json (permissions)</text>
      <text class="it" x="450" y="236">./scripts/setup.sh</text>
      <text class="ds" x="450" y="252">symlinks · repos/README.md · clones</text>
      <text class="it" x="450" y="280">check-service-access.sh</text>
      <text class="ds" x="450" y="296">→ .service-access.local.json (generated)</text>
      <text class="it" x="450" y="324">CONTEXT.md + symlinks (tracked, ready)</text>

      <path d="M390,180 C410,180 410,180 430,180" stroke="#7c83ff" stroke-width="2" fill="none"/>
      <text class="ds" x="410" y="172" text-anchor="middle" fill="#7c83ff">feeds</text>
    </svg>
    <div class="figcap">Figure 4 — Two setup layers. Authoritative tables: docs/workspace-setup.md</div>
  </div>

  <!-- ============================================= -->
  <h2 id="tracked">05 · What's tracked vs. gitignored</h2>
  <p>Nothing secret ever enters git. Credentials stay in the keychain; the repo holds only templates and
  non-secret identifiers. Full table in <code>docs/workspace-structure.md → “Gitignored vs. Checked In”</code>.</p>

  <table>
    <thead><tr><th>Path</th><th>In git?</th><th>Why</th></tr></thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr><td><code>CONTEXT.md</code> + CLAUDE/AGENTS/GEMINI symlinks</td><td class="yes">Yes</td><td>Agent entrypoints</td></tr>
      <tr><td><code>docs/ skills/ work/ prompt-library/ scripts/</code></td><td class="yes">Yes</td><td>Shared docs, skills, state, tooling</td></tr>
      <tr><td><code>opencode.json .opencode/ .gemini/settings.json .claude/settings.json</code></td><td class="yes">Yes</td><td>Shared per-runtime config + hook wiring</td></tr>
      <tr><td><code>*.example</code> (env, mcp, claude settings)</td><td class="yes">Yes</td><td>Templates to copy locally</td></tr>
      <tr><td><code>repos/README.md references/README.md</code></td><td class="yes">Yes</td><td>Registries (repos/README is a symlink)</td></tr>
      <tr><td><code>.env .mcp.json .claude/settings.local.json</code></td><td class="no">No</td><td>User-specific / may hold paths</td></tr>
      <tr><td><code>repos/* references/*</code> (except READMEs)</td><td class="no">No</td><td>Separate cloned repos</td></tr>
      <tr><td><code>.service-access.local.json</code></td><td class="no">No</td><td>Machine credential cache</td></tr>
      <tr><td><code>graphify-out/ temp/ tmp/ .DS_Store</code></td><td class="no">No</td><td>Locally regenerated / scratch</td></tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

  <!-- ============================================= -->
  <h2 id="toolchain">06 · Recommended toolchain</h2>
  <p>Optional external tools this workflow assumes. They're <strong>global / per-user</strong> — installed
  once per machine, never committed to the repo — except for two that also need a <strong>per-repo</strong>
  step. Full setup in <a href="recommended-tooling.md">docs/recommended-tooling.md</a>.</p>

  <div class="fig">

      <text class="ii" x="528" y="168">graphify graph</text>

      <text class="dd" x="528" y="232">↳ template already ships the</text>
      <text class="dd" x="528" y="248">  Gemini hook + OpenCode plugin</text>
    </svg>
    <div class="figcap">Figure 5 — Global install vs. per-repo setup. Detail in docs/recommended-tooling.md</div>
  </div>

  <p>Quick reference:</p>
  <pre><code># status line
npm install -g ccstatusline           # install once; wire statusLine in ~/.claude/settings.json

# plugins (official marketplace is built in)
claude plugin install superpowers@claude-plugins-official

# graphify
uv tool install "graphifyy[mcp]"
/graphify                             # build the graph for THIS repo → graphify-out/
graphify update .                     # refresh after code changes

# YouTube transcript MCP
yt-dlp --version                      # install via brew / apt / winget if missing
scripts/mcp/youtube-transcript.sh     # workspace-local stdio server

# skill repos — managed via agent-context (clone, then link with agent-context-sync)
git clone https://github.com/mattpocock/skills.git ~/Developer/references/mattpocock-skills

exec
/bin/zsh -lc "sed -n '421,900p' docs/setup-guide.html" in /Users/kashif/Developer/experiments/ai-workspace-template
 succeeded in 0ms:
git clone https://github.com/ForrestChang/andrej-karpathy-skills.git ~/Developer/references/karpathy-claude-md
agent-context-sync                    # links new skills into ~/.config/agent-context/skills

# Matt Pocock per-repo config (before to-tickets / triage / to-spec)
# run the setup-matt-pocock-skills skill in the repo</code></pre>

  <!-- ============================================= -->
  <h2 id="agents">07 · For AI agents</h2>
  <div class="callout ai">
    <h3>If you are an AI agent setting this up</h3>
    <ul class="tight">
      <li><strong>Read <code>CONTEXT.md</code> first</strong> — it's the front door and loads the project's
      conventions and the “Agent Coding Principles” / “Agent Context Discipline” rules.</li>
      <li><strong>Treat <code>docs/workspace-structure.md</code> as the spec.</strong> Its
      <em>“Agent Bootstrap Instructions”</em> section is a step-by-step procedure for scaffolding or
      extending a workspace — files to create, stub-header format, and post-scaffold verification checks.</li>
      <li><strong>Confirm preconditions before creating files</strong> — workspace name, repo list,
      editors/runtimes, and external services. Don't invent repo names or clone URLs.</li>
      <li><strong>Persist intermediate state to <code>work/&lt;project&gt;/</code></strong> so it
      survives context compaction — not to conversation memory.</li>
      <li><strong>Disk is the source of truth.</strong> Re-read files before asserting facts; verify
      symlinks resolve and scripts are executable after scaffolding.</li>
    </ul>
  </div>

  <div class="callout">
    <h3>Verify before you call it done</h3>
    <pre><code>gh auth status                                    # global: GitHub auth
claude mcp list                                   # local:  core MCP (graphify) visible
scripts/mcp/youtube-transcript.sh                 # local:  starts YouTube MCP (opt-in fragment)
./scripts/check-workspace-structure.sh            # local:  symlinks + dirs OK</code></pre>
  </div>

+<!doctype html>
+<html lang="en">

+<main>
+  <p class="eyebrow">AI Workspace Template</p>
+  <h1>Set up your project workspace</h1>
+  <p class="lede">A reusable, agent-aware development workspace — one shared context for Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode, and Gemini. This guide takes you from a fresh clone to a working project, whether you’re a human or an AI agent.</p>
+  <p><span class="tag">Claude Code</span> <span class="tag">Codex</span> <span class="tag">OpenCode</span> <span class="tag">Gemini</span> <span class="tag info">graphify MCP (core)</span> <span class="tag ok">gh CLI (required)</span> <span class="tag warn">MCP fragments: YouTube (opt-in)</span></p>
+
+  <nav class="toc"><ol>
+    <li><a href="#what">What you get</a></li>
+    <li><a href="#model">The mental model</a></li>
+    <li><a href="#quickstart">Quick start</a></li>
+    <li><a href="#global">Global vs local</a></li>
+    <li><a href="#tracked">Tracked vs ignored</a></li>
+    <li><a href="#toolchain">Recommended toolchain</a></li>
+    <li><a href="#agents">For AI agents</a></li>
+  </ol></nav>
+
+  <section id="what">
+    <h2>01 · What you get</h2>
+    <p>The template is a coordination layer — not product code. It gives every agent and human a single known place to find context, conventions, and active work. Everything project-specific is a <code>&lt;…&gt;</code> placeholder or a stub with a header comment, so you fill in, you don’t reverse-engineer.</p>
+    <figure>
+      <svg class="diagram" viewBox="0 0 580 430" role="img" aria-label="Top-level workspace directory structure showing context, documentation, skills, active work, repositories, scripts, local files, and runtime configuration">
+        <rect x="10" y="10" width="560" height="410" rx="10"/>
+        <text class="accent" x="30" y="40">&lt;workspace&gt;/</text>
+        <text x="48" y="70">CONTEXT.md</text><text class="small" x="210" y="70">← master context, the front door (read first)</text>
+        <text x="48" y="94">CLAUDE.md · AGENTS.md · GEMINI.md</text><text class="small" x="360" y="94">→ symlinks to CONTEXT.md</text>
+        <text x="48" y="118">README.md · SPEC.md</text><text class="small" x="210" y="118">human pitch · product intent</text>
+        <text class="accent" x="48" y="154">docs/</text><text class="small" x="210" y="154">workspace docs (structure, setup, registries)</text>
+        <text x="48" y="178">repo-context/</text><text class="small" x="210" y="178">per-repo navigation + design docs</text>
+        <text class="accent" x="48" y="214">skills/</text><text class="small" x="210" y="214">reusable agent workflows (starts empty)</text>
+        <text class="accent" x="48" y="238">work/</text><text class="small" x="210" y="238">active project state — survives compaction</text>
+        <text class="accent" x="48" y="262">prompt-library/</text><text class="small" x="210" y="262">prompt contracts for repeatable outputs</text>
+        <text class="accent" x="48" y="298">repos/</text><text class="small" x="210" y="298">cloned product repos (gitignored)</text>
+        <text class="accent" x="48" y="322">references/</text><text class="small" x="210" y="322">cloned third-party repos (gitignored)</text>
+        <text class="accent" x="48" y="346">scripts/</text><text class="small" x="210" y="346">setup + validation scripts</text>
+        <text x="48" y="382">.env.example · .mcp.json.example</text><text class="small" x="360" y="382">copy → local, gitignored</text>
+        <text x="48" y="406">.claude/ · .gemini/ · .opencode/ · opencode.json</text><text class="small" x="420" y="406">per-runtime config</text>
+      </svg>
+      <figcaption>Figure 1 — Top-level layout. Full detail in docs/workspace-structure.md</figcaption>
+    </figure>
+  </section>
+
+  <section id="model">
+    <h2>02 · The mental model</h2>
+    <p>One file is the front door. The three agent entrypoints are <strong>symlinks</strong> to it, so every runtime reads the exact same context — edit once, all agents see it.</p>
+    <figure>
+      <svg class="diagram" viewBox="0 0 580 240" role="img" aria-label="CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, and GEMINI.md all point by symlink to CONTEXT.md, the single source of truth">
+        <rect x="20" y="15" width="170" height="42" rx="6"/><text x="105" y="36">CLAUDE.md</text>
+        <rect x="20" y="99" width="170" height="42" rx="6"/><text x="105" y="120">AGENTS.md</text>
+        <rect x="20" y="183" width="170" height="42" rx="6"/><text x="105" y="204">GEMINI.md</text>
+        <rect class="solid info" x="385" y="88" width="175" height="70" rx="10"/><text class="inverse" x="472" y="115">CONTEXT.md</text><text class="small inverse" x="472" y="138">single source of truth</text>
+        <line class="arrow" x1="190" y1="36" x2="384" y2="105"/><line class="arrow" x1="190" y1="120" x2="384" y2="123"/><line class="arrow" x1="190" y1="204" x2="384" y2="141"/>
+        <text class="small" x="275" y="65">symlink</text>
+      </svg>
+      <figcaption>Figure 2 — Claude Code, Codex (AGENTS.md), and Gemini all resolve to one file</figcaption>
+    </figure>
+  </section>
+
+  <section id="quickstart">
+    <h2>03 · Quick start</h2>
+    <p>Five steps from clone to a working workspace. Steps 1–3 are mechanical; step 4 is where you make it <em>your</em> project.</p>
+    <figure>
+      <svg class="diagram" viewBox="0 0 580 170" role="img" aria-label="Setup flow from clone and re-initialize through bootstrap, wire agents, fill placeholders, and build">
+        <rect x="10" y="55" width="100" height="62" rx="8"/><text class="small" x="60" y="76">01</text><text x="60" y="98">Clone &amp;</text><text x="60" y="114">re-init</text>
+        <rect x="125" y="55" width="100" height="62" rx="8"/><text class="small" x="175" y="76">02</text><text x="175" y="98">Bootstrap</text>
+        <rect x="240" y="55" width="100" height="62" rx="8"/><text class="small" x="290" y="76">03</text><text x="290" y="98">Wire agents</text>
+        <rect x="355" y="55" width="100" height="62" rx="8"/><text class="small" x="405" y="76">04</text><text x="405" y="98">Fill</text><text x="405" y="114">placeholders</text>
+        <rect class="ok" x="470" y="55" width="100" height="62" rx="8"/><text class="small ok" x="520" y="76">05</text><text x="520" y="98">Build</text>
+        <line class="arrow" x1="110" y1="86" x2="124" y2="86"/><line class="arrow" x1="225" y1="86" x2="239" y2="86"/><line class="arrow" x1="340" y1="86" x2="354" y2="86"/><line class="arrow" x1="455" y1="86" x2="469" y2="86"/>
+      </svg>
+      <figcaption>Figure 3 — The setup pipeline</figcaption>
+    </figure>
+    <ol class="steps">
+      <li><h3>Create your workspace from the template</h3><p>On GitHub, click <strong>“Use this template”</strong>, or clone and reset git history:</p><pre><code>git clone https://github.com/&lt;you&gt;/ai-workspace-template.git &lt;project&gt;-workspace
+cd &lt;project&gt;-workspace
+rm -rf .git &amp;&amp; git init</code></pre></li>
+      <li><h3>Bootstrap the local files</h3><p>Run the setup script — it runs a dependency preflight, then creates <code>.env</code>, the agent symlinks, <code>repos/README.md</code>, and the per-user config copies (idempotent). Add <code>--clone-repos</code> for a multi-repo workspace. Missing tools? Follow <a href="../../../../../docs/runbooks/dependencies.md">runbooks/dependencies.md</a>.</p><pre><code>./scripts/setup.sh                 # creates .env, symlinks, repos/README.md
+./scripts/check-workspace-structure.sh   # verify symlinks resolve, dirs present</code></pre></li>
+      <li><h3>Wire up agents &amp; MCP</h3><p>Copy the per-user config templates (all gitignored), then authenticate. Run the check, then follow <a href="../../../../../docs/runbooks/authentication.md">runbooks/authentication.md</a> for anything missing; per-runtime MCP detail is in <code>docs/mcp-setup.md</code>. The YouTube transcript MCP server is workspace-local and credential-free; install <code>yt-dlp</code> if you use it.</p><pre><code>cp -n .env.example .env
+cp -n .mcp.json.example .mcp.json
+cp -n .claude/settings.json.example .claude/settings.local.json
+./scripts/check-service-access.sh                  # reports gaps + fix commands
+gh auth login                                      # if not already authenticated</code></pre></li>
+      <li><h3>Make it your project</h3><p>Replace every <code>&lt;…&gt;</code> placeholder and resolve the <code>TODO</code> / “Fill in” comments — start with <code>CONTEXT.md</code> (it loads into every agent session). Find what’s left:</p><pre><code>grep -rIn --exclude-dir=.git -e '&lt;[a-z-]\+&gt;' -e 'TODO' -e 'Fill in:' .</code></pre><ul><li><strong>CONTEXT.md</strong> — name, purpose, single- vs multi-repo model</li><li><strong>docs/repos-registry.md</strong> — one entry per product repo</li><li><strong>docs/service-access.md</strong> — your GitHub username; add a section per service</li><li><strong>SPEC.md</strong> — product intent (or delete for a pure coordination layer)</li><li><strong>work/</strong> — prune the maintainer’s shipped work history (<code>git rm -r work/*/</code>); it’s a worked example, not machinery — see <a href="../../../../../docs/template-usage.md">template-usage.md</a> §5</li></ul></li>
+      <li><h3>Start building</h3><p>Decide on the optional bits: <strong>graphify</strong> is the core MCP server (wired by default; it also adds <code>.gemini/settings.json</code> / <code>.opencode/plugins/graphify.js</code>). <strong>YouTube transcript</strong> MCP is an opt-in fragment under <code>mcp-fragments/</code>, loaded per session — see <code>docs/mcp-setup.md</code>; GitHub goes through the required <code>gh</code> CLI (no MCP server). Delete what you don’t use. Add reusable workflows under <code>skills/</code> and list them in <code>CONTEXT.md</code>. The template ships four: <code>checkpoint</code> (session wrap-up), <code>onboard-repo</code> (bring a repo in), <code>rlm</code> (query huge contexts), and <code>decision-log</code> — capture the <em>why</em> behind a decision (<code>/decision</code>) as an ephemeral note in <code>work/&lt;proj&gt;/decisions.md</code>, promoted to a committed ADR under <code>docs/adr/</code> for lasting-weight calls. See the <strong>Decision Records</strong> section of <code>CONTEXT.md</code>. <strong>Checks:</strong> <code>scripts/run-checks.sh</code> runs every workspace check; on GitHub, <code>.github/workflows/checks.yml</code> runs it on each push (delete the file to opt out), and <code>git config core.hooksPath scripts/git-hooks</code> opts you in to a pre-commit run of the fast set.</p></li>
+    </ol>
+    <p>Deeper reference: <a href="../../../../../docs/template-usage.md">docs/template-usage.md</a> · <a href="../../../../../docs/workspace-setup.md">docs/workspace-setup.md</a> · <a href="../../../../../docs/workspace-structure.md">docs/workspace-structure.md</a></p>
+  </section>
+
+  <section id="global">
+    <h2>04 · Global vs. workspace-local setup</h2>
+    <p>Setup splits into two layers. Do the <span class="tag warn">GLOBAL</span> layer once per machine; the <span class="tag info">LOCAL</span> layer once per clone. The local layer depends on the global one (the MCP token and the <code>gh</code> login).</p>
+    <figure>
+      <svg class="diagram" viewBox="0 0 580 370" role="img" aria-label="Global machine configuration and workspace-local files are separate setup layers, with global configuration feeding local setup">
+        <rect class="warn" x="10" y="10" width="265" height="345" rx="10"/><text class="warn" x="142" y="35">GLOBAL — once per machine</text><text class="small" x="142" y="55">~/ home directory · OS keychain · never in git</text>
+        <text x="28" y="90">agent CLIs on PATH</text><text class="small" x="28" y="108">claude · codex · opencode · gemini</text>
+        <text x="28" y="140">gh CLI login (required)</text><text class="small" x="28" y="158">all GitHub operations · gh auth status</text>
+        <text x="28" y="210">~/.codex/config.toml</text><text class="small" x="28" y="228">Codex MCP registration (optional)</text>
+        <text x="28" y="262">~/.gemini/settings.json</text><text class="small" x="28" y="280">Gemini MCP registration (optional)</text>
+        <text x="28" y="330">OS keychain</text>
+        <rect class="info" x="305" y="10" width="265" height="345" rx="10"/><text class="info" x="437" y="35">LOCAL — once per clone</text><text class="small" x="437" y="55">inside the repo · copy the .example files</text>
+        <text x="323" y="90">.env.example → .env</text><text class="small" x="323" y="108">non-secret identifiers only</text>
+        <text x="323" y="140">.mcp.json.example → .mcp.json</text><text class="small" x="323" y="158">Claude Code project MCP</text>
+        <text x="323" y="190">.claude/settings.json.example</text><text class="small" x="323" y="208">→ .claude/settings.local.json (permissions)</text>
+        <text x="323" y="240">./scripts/setup.sh</text><text class="small" x="323" y="258">symlinks · repos/README.md · clones</text>
+        <text x="323" y="290">check-service-access.sh</text><text class="small" x="323" y="308">→ .service-access.local.json (generated)</text>
+        <text x="323" y="340">CONTEXT.md + symlinks (tracked, ready)</text>
+        <line class="arrow accent" x1="275" y1="180" x2="304" y2="180"/><text class="small accent" x="290" y="168">feeds</text>
+      </svg>
+      <figcaption>Figure 4 — Two setup layers. Authoritative tables: docs/workspace-setup.md</figcaption>
+    </figure>
+  </section>
+
+  <section id="tracked">
+    <h2>05 · What’s tracked vs. gitignored</h2>
+    <p>Nothing secret ever enters git. Credentials stay in the keychain; the repo holds only templates and non-secret identifiers. Full table in <code>docs/workspace-structure.md → “Gitignored vs. Checked In”</code>.</p>
+    <table><thead><tr><th>Path</th><th>In git?</th><th>Why</th></tr></thead><tbody>
+      <tr><td><code>CONTEXT.md</code> + CLAUDE/AGENTS/GEMINI symlinks</td><td>Yes</td><td>Agent entrypoints</td></tr>
+      <tr><td><code>docs/ skills/ work/ prompt-library/ scripts/</code></td><td>Yes</td><td>Shared docs, skills, state, tooling</td></tr>
+      <tr><td><code>opencode.json .opencode/ .gemini/settings.json .claude/settings.json</code></td><td>Yes</td><td>Shared per-runtime config + hook wiring</td></tr>
+      <tr><td><code>*.example</code> (env, mcp, claude settings)</td><td>Yes</td><td>Templates to copy locally</td></tr>
+      <tr><td><code>repos/README.md references/README.md</code></td><td>Yes</td><td>Registries (repos/README is a symlink)</td></tr>
+      <tr><td><code>.env .mcp.json .claude/settings.local.json</code></td><td>No</td><td>User-specific / may hold paths</td></tr>
+      <tr><td><code>repos/* references/*</code> (except READMEs)</td><td>No</td><td>Separate cloned repos</td></tr>
+      <tr><td><code>.service-access.local.json</code></td><td>No</td><td>Machine credential cache</td></tr>
+      <tr><td><code>graphify-out/ temp/ tmp/ .DS_Store</code></td><td>No</td><td>Locally regenerated / scratch</td></tr>
+    </tbody></table>
+  </section>
+
+  <section id="toolchain">
+    <h2>06 · Recommended toolchain</h2>
+    <p>Optional external tools this workflow assumes. They’re <strong>global / per-user</strong> — installed once per machine, never committed to the repo — except for two that also need a <strong>per-repo</strong> step. Full setup in <a href="../../../../../docs/recommended-tooling.md">docs/recommended-tooling.md</a>.</p>
+    <figure>
+      <svg class="diagram" viewBox="0 0 580 310" role="img" aria-label="Recommended toolchain separates global per-user tools from per-repository configuration for Matt Pocock skills and graphify">
+        <rect class="warn" x="10" y="10" width="335" height="285" rx="10"/><text class="warn" x="177" y="35">GLOBAL — once per machine</text>
+        <text x="28" y="68">Status line</text><text class="small" x="28" y="85">ccstatusline · ~/.config + ~/.claude/settings.json</text>
+        <text x="28" y="118">superpowers + plugins</text><text class="small" x="28" y="135">claude plugin install …@claude-plugins-official</text>
+        <text x="28" y="168">Matt Pocock skills</text><text class="small" x="28" y="185">tdd · diagnosing-bugs · triage · to-tickets · … (agent-context)</text>
+        <text x="28" y="218">Karpathy principles</text><text class="small" x="28" y="235">4 principles already in CONTEXT.md · karpathy-examples</text>
+        <text x="28" y="268">graphify CLI</text><text class="small" x="28" y="285">uv tool install “graphifyy[mcp]” · yt-dlp for YouTube MCP</text>
+        <rect class="info" x="365" y="10" width="205" height="285" rx="10"/><text class="info" x="467" y="35">PER-REPO step</text>
+        <text x="382" y="75">Matt Pocock config</text><text class="small" x="382" y="92">run setup-matt-pocock-skills</text><text class="small" x="382" y="109">→ ## Agent skills block +</text><text class="small" x="382" y="126">docs/agents/*.md</text>
+        <text x="382" y="166">graphify graph</text><text class="small" x="382" y="183">/graphify (build graphify-out/)</text><text class="small" x="382" y="200">graphify update . (refresh)</text><text class="small" x="382" y="235">↳ template already ships the</text><text class="small" x="382" y="252">Gemini hook + OpenCode plugin</text>
+      </svg>
+      <figcaption>Figure 5 — Global install vs. per-repo setup. Detail in docs/recommended-tooling.md</figcaption>
+    </figure>
+    <p>Quick reference:</p>
+    <pre><code># status line
+npm install -g ccstatusline           # install once; wire statusLine in ~/.claude/settings.json
+
+# plugins (official marketplace is built in)
+claude plugin install superpowers@claude-plugins-official
+
+# graphify
+uv tool install "graphifyy[mcp]"
+/graphify                             # build the graph for THIS repo → graphify-out/
+graphify update .                     # refresh after code changes
+
+# YouTube transcript MCP
+yt-dlp --version                      # install via brew / apt / winget if missing
+scripts/mcp/youtube-transcript.sh     # workspace-local stdio server
+
+# skill repos — managed via agent-context (clone, then link with agent-context-sync)
+git clone https://github.com/mattpocock/skills.git ~/Developer/references/mattpocock-skills
+git clone https://github.com/ForrestChang/andrej-karpathy-skills.git ~/Developer/references/karpathy-claude-md
+agent-context-sync                    # links new skills into ~/.config/agent-context/skills
+
+# Matt Pocock per-repo config (before to-tickets / triage / to-spec)
+# run the setup-matt-pocock-skills skill in the repo</code></pre>
+  </section>
+
+  <section id="agents">
+    <h2>07 · For AI agents</h2>
+    <div class="callout"><h3>If you are an AI agent setting this up</h3><ul>
+      <li><strong>Read <code>CONTEXT.md</code> first</strong> — it’s the front door and loads the project’s conventions and the “Agent Coding Principles” / “Agent Context Discipline” rules.</li>
+      <li><strong>Treat <code>docs/workspace-structure.md</code> as the spec.</strong> Its <em>“Agent Bootstrap Instructions”</em> section is a step-by-step procedure for scaffolding or extending a workspace — files to create, stub-header format, and post-scaffold verification checks.</li>
+      <li><strong>Confirm preconditions before creating files</strong> — workspace name, repo list, editors/runtimes, and external services. Don’t invent repo names or clone URLs.</li>
+      <li><strong>Persist intermediate state to <code>work/&lt;project&gt;/</code></strong> so it survives context compaction — not to conversation memory.</li>
+      <li><strong>Disk is the source of truth.</strong> Re-read files before asserting facts; verify symlinks resolve and scripts are executable after scaffolding.</li>
+    </ul></div>
+    <div class="callout"><h3>Verify before you call it done</h3><pre><code>gh auth status                                    # global: GitHub auth
+claude mcp list                                   # local:  core MCP (graphify) visible
+scripts/mcp/youtube-transcript.sh                 # local:  starts YouTube MCP (opt-in fragment)
+./scripts/check-workspace-structure.sh            # local:  symlinks + dirs OK</code></pre></div>
+  </section>
+</main>
